<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409" about="#mwt10" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;templatestyles&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;src&quot;:&quot;Plainlist/styles.css&quot;}}"><div class="plainlist ">
<ul><li><b>Boris</b>:</li>
<li>19 November 2012<span style="display:none">(2012-11-19)</span> (aged<span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span>79)</li>
<li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg" title="Saint Petersburg">Saint Petersburg</a>, Russia</li></ul>

</div><span style="display:none" data-plural="0"></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Occupation</th><td class="infobox-data role">Writers</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" career</b></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Genres</th><td class="infobox-data"><span class="mw-empty-elt"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1374427404" fiction">Science fiction</a></li><li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophical_fiction" title="Philosophical fiction">philosophical fiction</a></li><li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absurdist_fiction" title="Absurdist fiction">absurdist fiction</a></li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Notable<span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span>awards</th><td class="infobox-data"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aelita_Prize" title="Aelita Prize">Aelita Prize</a> 1981</td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
</td></tr></tbody></table><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt5"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_hCards"></span>

<p id="mwCA">The brothers <b id="mwCQ">Arkady Strugatsky</b> (28 August 1925 – 12 October 1991) and <b id="mwCg">Boris Strugatsky</b> (14 April 1933 – 19 November 2012) were Soviet and Russian <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science-fiction_author" title="Science-fiction author" class="mw-redirect" id="mwCw">science-fiction authors</a> who collaborated through most of their careers.</p>

<p id="mwDA">In the second half of the 1950s, military translator A. N. Strugatsky, with the assistance of journalist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lev_Petrov?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Lev Petrov (page does not exist)" about="#mwt13" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwDQ" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ill&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Ill&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Lev Petrov&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Петров, Лев Сергеевич&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Lev Petrov&quot;]}}">Lev Petrov</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt13" id="mwDg"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Петров,%20Лев%20Сергеевич" title="ru:Петров, Лев Сергеевич" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span> and writer and intelligence officer <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Kim_(writer)?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Roman Kim (writer) (page does not exist)" about="#mwt14" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwDw" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ill&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Ill&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roman Kim (writer)&quot;},&quot;lt&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Roman Kim&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ким, Роман Николаевич&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Roman Kim (writer)&quot;]}}">Roman Kim</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt14" id="mwEA"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ким,%20Роман%20Николаевич" title="ru:Ким, Роман Николаевич" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span>, published the documentary novella <i id="mwEQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashes_of_Bikini?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Ashes of Bikini (page does not exist)" about="#mwt15" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwEg" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ill&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Ill&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ashes of Bikini&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Пепел Бикини&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Ashes of Bikini&quot;]}}">Ashes of Bikini</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt15" id="mwEw"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Пепел%20Бикини" title="ru:Пепел Бикини" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span></i> (journal versions in 1956 and 1957, book edition in 1958) and secured a position as an editor at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khudozhestvennaya_Literatura" title="Khudozhestvennaya Literatura" id="mwFA">Goslitizdat</a>. B. N. Strugatsky, who worked at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulkovo_Observatory" title="Pulkovo Observatory" id="mwFQ">Pulkovo Observatory</a>, also harbored literary ambitions; according to legend, the brothers decided to write together on a bet. Between 1957 and 1959, Arkady and Boris Strugatsky wrote the novella <i id="mwFg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Land_of_Crimson_Clouds" title="The Land of Crimson Clouds" id="mwFw">The Land of Crimson Clouds</a></i> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short_stories_by_the_Strugatsky_brothers" title="Short stories by the Strugatsky brothers" id="mwGA">several short stories</a>, which immediately attracted attention. In 1964, the Strugatskys were admitted to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Writers_of_the_RSFSR" title="Union of Writers of the RSFSR" class="mw-redirect" id="mwGQ">Union of Writers of the RSFSR</a>. After years of experimentation, they developed a working method that involved not only joint discussion of ideas but also the oral rehearsal of every sentence. The writing process followed a detailed plan, which was developed in advance and discussed multiple times.</p>

<p id="mwGg">Starting with works in the synthetic genre of adventure and scientific-technical fiction, the Strugatskys quickly transitioned to social prognostics and modeling in the form of "realistic fiction," with ideological content wrapped in a gripping plot. Most of their books explore the establishment of contact with alien intelligence, the question of the permissibility and justification of intervention or non-intervention in the natural evolution of civilizations of any type, and the study of various forms of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utopia" title="Utopia" id="mwGw">utopia</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dystopia" title="Dystopia" id="mwHA">dystopia</a>.</p>

<p id="mwHQ">Significant attention in their work was devoted to the problem of the ideologization and de-ideologization of society and the role of culture in the state. In the first half of the 1960s, the Strugatskys created a unified fictional universe, conventionally called the <i id="mwHg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noon_Universe" title="Noon Universe" id="mwHw">Noon Universe</a></i>, which serves as the setting for nearly a dozen novellas. The image of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism" title="Communism" id="mwIA">communism</a> they constructed evolved toward permanent <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geopolitics" title="Geopolitics" id="mwIQ">geopolitical</a> and cosmic expansion and associated mechanisms of social control. Their exploration of various forms of utopia led the Strugatskys (starting with <i id="mwIg">The Far Rainbow</i>) to the conviction that humanity would inevitably split into unequal <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_stratification" title="Social stratification" id="mwIw">strata</a>, not all of whose members are suitable or worthy of entering a bright future. The prospect of creating a biological civilization that radically reconstructs human nature and opposes technical culture also concerned the co-authors. From the 1980s, B. N. Strugatsky began to reassess their joint creative path in the context of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism_in_Russia" title="Liberalism in Russia" id="mwJA">liberalism</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissident_movement_in_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissident movement in the Soviet Union" class="mw-redirect" id="mwJQ">dissidence</a>.</p>

<p id="mwJg">Having achieved significant fame in the 1960s, the Strugatskys faced persecution of philosophical fiction in the USSR by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agitprop" title="Agitprop" id="mwJw">Department of Agitation and Propaganda of the Central Committee of the CPSU</a> and the leadership of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komsomol" title="Komsomol" id="mwKA">All-Union Leninist Communist Youth League</a>. In the 1970s and the first half of the 1980s, the number of publications and reprints decreased, and several lengthy texts gained semi-banned status, circulating in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samizdat" title="Samizdat" id="mwKQ">samizdat</a> (e.g., <i id="mwKg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ugly_Swans" title="The Ugly Swans" id="mwKw">The Ugly Swans</a></i>). Based on the novella <i id="mwLA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roadside_Picnic" title="Roadside Picnic" id="mwLQ">Roadside Picnic</a></i>, which had no book editions at the time, the Strugatskys wrote the screenplay for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Arsenyevich_Tarkovsky" title="Andrei Arsenyevich Tarkovsky" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLg">A. Tarkovsky</a>'s film <i id="mwLw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stalker_(1979_film)" title="Stalker (1979 film)" id="mwMA">Stalker</a></i> (1979).</p>

<p id="mwMQ">In the 1980s, the Strugatskys became some of the most published Soviet writers, a symbol of independent thought, and were awarded the RSFSR State Prize named after M. Gorky (1986). Yet, the Strugatskys took Marxism seriously and were hardly dissidents. While Arkady insisted in the mid-1980s that he had never doubted the truthfulness of communist ideals (“I have imbibed them from my childhood”), Boris wrote in 1994 that humanity had not invented anything “more radiant, just and attractive” than communism.<sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-1&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwMg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNA">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwNg">Between 1991 and 1994, the publishing house <i id="mwNw">Tekst</i> released the first collected works of the Strugatskys. In the 1990s, numerous editions were published, including the series <i id="mwOA">Worlds of the Strugatsky Brothers</i>. A group of Strugatsky researchers (the so-called "Ludeny Group") published an 11-volume collected works based on archival texts between 2001 and 2003, and a complete 33-volume collected works between 2015 and 2022.</p>

<p id="mwOQ">The Strugatskys' work significantly influenced the spread of dissent among the Soviet <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligentsia" title="Intelligentsia" id="mwOg">intelligentsia</a> in the 1970s and 1980s, and was studied by literary scholars, social philosophers, and political scientists due to its interest in ideological and literary constructs.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwOw" data-mw="{&quot;autoGenerated&quot;:true}"></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwPA" aria-labelledby="Life_and_work"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Life_and_work">Life and work</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Life and work"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwPQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Amazing_science_fiction_stories_195905.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwPg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Amazing_science_fiction_stories_195905.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Amazing_science_fiction_stories_195905.jpg/250px-Amazing_science_fiction_stories_195905.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/01/Amazing_science_fiction_stories_195905.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" data-file-width="400" data-file-height="558" data-file-type="bitmap" height="349" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwPw"></a><figcaption id="mwQA">A translated Strugatsky story appeared in <i id="mwQQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazing_Stories" title="Amazing Stories" id="mwQg">Amazing Stories</a></i> in 1959</figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwQw">The Strugatsky brothers (<span title="Russian-language text" about="#mwt19" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwRA" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;lang&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Lang&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;братья Стругацкие&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span lang="ru">братья Стругацкие</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Russian-language_text" about="#mwt19" id="mwRQ"> or simply <span title="Russian-language text" about="#mwt20" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwRg" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;lang&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Lang&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Стругацкие&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><span lang="ru">Стругацкие</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Russian-language_text" about="#mwt20" id="mwRw">) were born to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natan_Strugatsky" title="Natan Strugatsky" id="mwSA">Natan Strugatsky</a>, an art critic, and his wife, a teacher. Their father was <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Jews" title="Russian Jews" class="mw-redirect" id="mwSQ">Jewish</a> and their mother was <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church" id="mwSg">Russian Orthodox</a>. Their early work was influenced by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Yefremov" title="Ivan Yefremov" id="mwSw">Ivan Yefremov</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanisław_Lem" title="Stanisław Lem" id="mwTA">Stanisław Lem</a>. Later they went on to develop their own, unique style of science fiction writing that emerged from the period of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_rationalism?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Soviet rationalism (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" id="mwTQ" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Soviet rationalism&quot;]}}">Soviet rationalism</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_literature" title="Soviet literature" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTg">Soviet literature</a> and evolved into novels interpreted as works of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_criticism" title="Social criticism" id="mwTw">social criticism</a>.<sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-2&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwUA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUg">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwVA">Their best-known novel, <i id="mwVQ">Piknik na obochine</i>,  has been translated into English as <i id="mwVg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roadside_Picnic" title="Roadside Picnic" id="mwVw">Roadside Picnic</a></i>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Tarkovsky" title="Andrei Tarkovsky" id="mwWA">Andrei Tarkovsky</a> adapted the novel for the screen as <i id="mwWQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stalker_(1979_film)" title="Stalker (1979 film)" id="mwWg">Stalker</a></i> (1979).</p>

<p id="mwWw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algis_Budrys" title="Algis Budrys" id="mwXA">Algis Budrys</a> compared their <i id="mwXQ">An Emergency Case</i> and Arkady's <i id="mwXg">Wanderers and Travellers</i> to the work of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eando_Binder" title="Eando Binder" id="mwXw">Eando Binder</a>.<sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-budrys196809_3-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;budrys196809&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-budrys196809-3&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-budrys196809-3" id="mwYA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwZA">In 1991, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_Publishers" title="Text Publishers" id="mwZQ">Text Publishers</a> brought out the collected works by Arkady and Boris Strugatsky.<sup about="#mwt29" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-4&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwZg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwZw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-5&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwag"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwaw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwbg" aria-labelledby="Arkady"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Arkady">Arkady</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Arkady"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwbw">Arkady Strugatsky was born 25 August 1925 in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batumi" title="Batumi" id="mwcA">Batumi</a>; the family later moved to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leningrad" title="Leningrad" class="mw-redirect" id="mwcQ">Leningrad</a>. In January 1942, Arkady and his father were evacuated from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Leningrad" title="Siege of Leningrad" id="mwcg">Siege of Leningrad</a>, but Arkady was the only survivor in his train car; his father died upon reaching <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vologda" title="Vologda" id="mwcw">Vologda</a>. Arkady was drafted into the Soviet army in 1943. He trained first at the artillery school in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aktyubinsk" title="Aktyubinsk" class="mw-redirect" id="mwdA">Aktyubinsk</a> and later at the Military Institute of Foreign Languages in Moscow, from which he graduated in 1949 as an interpreter of English and Japanese. He worked as a teacher and interpreter for the military until 1955. In 1955, he began working as an editor and writer. In 1958, he began collaborating with his brother Boris, a collaboration that lasted until Arkady's death on <span class="avoidwrap" style="display:inline-block;" about="#mwt33" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwdQ" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;awrap&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Awrap&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;12 October 1991&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}">12 October 1991</span>.<sup about="#mwt34" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-6&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwdg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mweA">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mweQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Arkady Strugatsky became a member of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Writers" title="Union of Soviet Writers" id="mweg">Union of Soviet Writers</a> in 1964. In addition to his own writing, he translated Japanese short stories and novels, as well as some English works with his brother.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwew" aria-labelledby="Boris"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Boris">Boris</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Boris"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwfA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Boris_Strugatsky_Seminar_20060109_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwfQ"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Boris_Strugatsky_Seminar_20060109_02.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Boris_Strugatsky_Seminar_20060109_02.jpg/250px-Boris_Strugatsky_Seminar_20060109_02.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/22/Boris_Strugatsky_Seminar_20060109_02.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="400" data-file-type="bitmap" height="333" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwfg"></a><figcaption id="mwfw">Boris Strugatsky in 2006</figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwgA">Born 14 April 1933, Boris Strugatsky remained in Leningrad with his mother during the siege of the city during World War II. He graduated from high school in 1950 and applied to the physics department at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leningrad_State_University" title="Leningrad State University" class="mw-redirect" id="mwgQ">Leningrad State University</a>, but studied astronomy instead. After graduating in 1955, he worked as an astronomer and computer engineer at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulkovo_Observatory" title="Pulkovo Observatory" id="mwgg">Pulkovo Observatory</a>. In 1960 he participated in a geodetic and astronomical expedition in the Caucasus. Boris Strugatsky became a member of the writers' union of the USSR in 1964. In 1966, he became a full-time writer.<sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-7&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwgw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhg">]</span></span></a></sup> From 1972 he acted as the head of the Leningrad seminar of young <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speculative_fiction" title="Speculative fiction" id="mwhw">speculative fiction</a> writers, which subsequently became known as the "Boris Strugatsky Seminar". He established the "Bronze Snail" literary prize. He was an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnostic" title="Agnostic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwiA">agnostic</a>.<sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-8&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwiQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwig"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjA">]</span></span></a></sup> After the death of his brother, he published two more novels under a pseudonym.  Boris Strugatsky died in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg" title="Saint Petersburg" id="mwjQ">Saint Petersburg</a> on 19 November 2012.<sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-9&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkA">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-10&quot;}}"><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwkg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwkw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlA">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwlg" aria-labelledby="Artistic_origins"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Artistic_origins">Artistic origins</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Artistic origins"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwlw" aria-labelledby="Cultural_and_socio-political_environment_of_the_Strugatsky_Brothers'_formation"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Cultural_and_socio-political_environment_of_the_Strugatsky_Brothers'_formation"><span id="Cultural_and_socio-political_environment_of_the_Strugatsky_Brothers.27_formation" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Cultural and socio-political environment of the Strugatsky Brothers' formation</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Cultural and socio-political environment of the Strugatsky Brothers' formation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwmA">A defining feature of the Strugatsky Brothers' work is its collaborative nature.<sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-13&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;refn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Refn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Biographers and critics have repeatedly noted the difference in quality between the Strugatskys' literary works created in various authorship formats. Ant Skalandis, characterizing the debut novella in three stories ''From the inside'' (1960), said it was written by three different people: the first chapter by Arkady Strugatsky, the second by Boris Strugatsky, and the third by \&quot;a very young, very inexperienced writer\&quot; — the Strugatsky Brothers.{{sfn|Skalandis|2008|p=202}} Boris Mezhyev also stated: \&quot;Everything written by S. Yaroslavtsev (i.e., [[Arkady Natanovich Strugatsky|Arkady Natanovich]] alone) was, on the whole, an order of magnitude lower than the work of {{abbr|ABS|Abbreviation used by the co-authors: \&quot;Arkady and Boris Strugatsky\&quot;.|0}}, while what was written by S. Vititsky (i.e., [[Boris Natanovich Strugatsky|Boris Natanovich]] alone) was no weaker than the best works of the great brothers. Ant Skalandis described the individual works of A. N. and B. N. Strugatsky in similar terms.{{sfn|Skalandis|2008|p=622}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-13" data-mw-group="Notes" id="mwmQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">[</span>Notes 1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnA">]</span></span></a></sup> Between 1941 and 1956, Arkady and Boris Strugatsky lived apart, not united by professional activities or daily life; their communication was primarily in written form. The circumstances shaping the personalities of the two brothers, as well as their socio-political and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aesthetics" title="Aesthetics" id="mwnQ">aesthetic</a> views, were entirely different.<sup about="#mwt53" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016114–115_14-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016114–115&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016114–115-14&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;114–115&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016114–115-14" id="mwng"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoA">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt56" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-15&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;refn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Refn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Boris Strugatsky also categorically stated that the eventful side of their biography with his brother concluded in 1956.The realization of a unified writerly persona prompted the brothers to calculate its \&quot;birth date,\&quot; equidistant from 1925 and 1933; they arrived at 1929.&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-15" data-mw-group="Notes" id="mwog"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwow"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpA">[</span>Notes 2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt59" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201236_16-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201236&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201236-16&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Volodihin&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Prashkevich&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;36&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201236-16" id="mwpg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwpw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqA">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqQ">]</span></span></a></sup> In particular, despite a relatively small age difference (about eight years), Arkady and Boris belonged to different generations in terms of worldview: in 1941, the elder brother was 16 years old, and his childhood coincided with an era "for which it was customary to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/&quot;Thank_you,_Comrade_Stalin,_for_our_happy_childhood!&quot;?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="&quot;Thank you, Comrade Stalin, for our happy childhood!&quot; (page does not exist)" about="#mwt62" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwqg" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Interlanguage link&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Interlanguage_link&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;\&quot;Thank you, Comrade Stalin, for our happy childhood!\&quot;&quot;},&quot;lt&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;thank to Comrade Stalin&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Спасибо товарищу Сталину за наше счастливое детство!&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;\&quot;Thank you, Comrade Stalin, for our happy childhood!\&quot;&quot;]}}">thank to Comrade Stalin</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt62" id="mwqw"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Спасибо%20товарищу%20Сталину%20за%20наше%20счастливое%20детство!" title="ru:Спасибо товарищу Сталину за наше счастливое детство!" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span>. Without any irony or reservations in this case: a peaceful, calm life in a good family, among kind friends, in a wonderful city, in a beloved country that both adults and children genuinely took pride in".<sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis200839_17-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis200839&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200839-17&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;39&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200839-17" id="mwrA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrw">]</span></span></a></sup> Boris Strugatsky's formative years coincided with the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Patriotic_War_(term)" title="Great Patriotic War (term)" id="mwsA">war</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Leningrad" title="Siege of Leningrad" id="mwsQ">blockade</a>, and evacuation, which predetermined negative memories of his childhood and youth and a skeptical attitude toward the country and its political system.<sup about="#mwt66" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016116–117_18-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016116–117&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016116–117-18&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;116–117&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016116–117-18" id="mwsg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwtg">The socio-political preferences of the Strugatsky Brothers were shaped in entirely different environments. Arkady Strugatsky, having graduated from the Military Institute of Foreign Languages, became a translator officer proficient in Japanese and English, and from 1946 to 1954, he gained experience working with classified documents and interrogating prisoners of war and war criminals. Boris Strugatsky, after returning to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leningrad" title="Leningrad" class="mw-redirect" id="mwtw">Leningrad</a>, led the ordinary life of an intellectual schoolboy and student, graduating from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg_State_University" title="Saint Petersburg State University" id="mwuA">Leningrad University</a> (Faculty of Mathematics and Mechanics). Their life-meaning orientations converged under the influence of constant correspondence and rare meetings during Arkady's leaves, which were spent in Leningrad. After the elder brother's demobilization in 1956, he settled in Moscow, and meetings and correspondence with his brother became much more frequent, though their main circles of friends and acquaintances remained different until the end of their lives, despite belonging to the Soviet scientific-technical and artistic elite (including Naum Korzhavin and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Vysotsky" title="Vladimir Vysotsky" id="mwuQ">Vladimir Vysotsky</a>). The path of both brothers into literature coincided with the period of the Thaw and the scientific-technical romanticism it engendered, which was highly fruitful for social engineering.<sup about="#mwt69" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016154–158_19-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016154–158&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016154–158-19&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;154–158&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016154–158-19" id="mwug"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwuw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwvg">The Strugatskys' reading preferences, which provided them with pretexts and models for developing their own literary style, were distinctive. Arkady and Boris Strugatsky were interested in philosophy, studying the works of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism-Leninism" title="Marxism-Leninism" class="mw-redirect" id="mwvw">Marxist–Leninist</a> classics not only for professional or academic purposes. Both valued Russian classical literature, stating that they learned to write from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolai_Vasilyevich_Gogol" title="Nikolai Vasilyevich Gogol" class="mw-redirect" id="mwwA">N. V. Gogol</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Tolstoy" title="Leo Tolstoy" id="mwwQ">L. N. Tolstoy</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksey_Nikolayevich_Tolstoy" title="Aleksey Nikolayevich Tolstoy" id="mwwg">A. N. Tolstoy</a>.<sup about="#mwt72" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016158–159_20-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016158–159&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016158–159-20&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;158–159&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016158–159-20" id="mwww"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxQ">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">]</span></span></a></sup> Arkady Strugatsky, fluent in Japanese and English, professionally followed literary novelties for many years; he likely read <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Orwell" title="George Orwell" id="mwxw">Orwell</a> early on. He had access to many Japanese authors in their original language, including <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryunosuke_Akutagawa" title="Ryunosuke Akutagawa" class="mw-redirect" id="mwyA">Akutagawa</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kan_Kikuchi" title="Kan Kikuchi" id="mwyQ">Kan Kikuchi</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takeo_Arishima" title="Takeo Arishima" id="mwyg">Arishima Takeo</a>. At the same time, A. Strugatsky was indifferent to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_poetry" title="Japanese poetry" id="mwyw">Japanese poetry</a>, and all epigraphs and quotes from Japanese authors were selected by Boris Natanovich.<sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016152_21-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016152&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016152-21&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;152&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016152-21" id="mwzA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzg">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzw">]</span></span></a></sup> Both brothers, from childhood, loved <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventure_literature" title="Adventure literature" class="mw-redirect" id="mw0A">adventure literature</a> and science fiction, favoring foreign authors. Most books by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudyard_Kipling" title="Rudyard Kipling" id="mw0Q">Kipling</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Dumas,_père" title="Alexandre Dumas, père" class="mw-redirect" id="mw0g">Dumas</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H._G._Wells" title="H. G. Wells" id="mw0w">Wells</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Jacolliot" title="Louis Jacolliot" id="mw1A">Jacolliot</a>, and other writers were impossible to obtain in stores or public libraries, coming instead from the book collections of their parents or friends.<sup about="#mwt78" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis200828_22-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis200828&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200828-22&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;28&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200828-22" id="mw1Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw1g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1w">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt81" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201234_23-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201234&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201234-23&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Volodihin&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Prashkevich&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;34&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2012&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVolodihinPrashkevich201234-23" id="mw2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2w">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt84" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016147_24-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016147&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016147-24&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;147&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016147-24" id="mw3Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw3g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3w">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt87" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKukulin_25-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKukulin&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKukulin-25&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Kukulin&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKukulin-25" id="mw4Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw4g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4w">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mw5Q" aria-labelledby="Work_in_tandem"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Work_in_tandem">Work in tandem</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arkady_and_Boris_Strugatsky&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Work in tandem"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mw5g">According to Arkady Strugatsky's recollections, his father, Natan Zalmanovich, introduced him to literature, including science fiction, by telling him an "endless novel, created by him based on the plots of books by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Mayne_Reid" title="Thomas Mayne Reid" id="mw5w">Mayne Reid</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Verne" title="Jules Verne" id="mw6A">Jules Verne</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Fenimore_Cooper" title="James Fenimore Cooper" id="mw6Q">Fenimore Cooper</a>". The elder Strugatsky brother began attempting to write science fiction prose as early as the late 1930s (according to B. N. Strugatsky, this was the novella "Major Kovalev's Find," lost during the Leningrad blockade). Such plots were composed orally and discussed with his friend and neighbor Igor Ashmarin. According to Mikhail Shavshin, Ashmarin's personality likely had a significant influence on at least the elder brother's life, as he served as a prototype for characters in many literary works.<sup about="#mwt90" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShavshin2015220_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEShavshin2015220&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEShavshin2015220-26&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Shavshin&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2015&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;220&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShavshin2015220-26" id="mw6g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7A">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7Q">]</span></span></a></sup> Occasionally, the younger brother Boris was admitted to this "literary feast".<sup about="#mwt93" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis200830_27-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis200830&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200830-27&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;30&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200830-27" id="mw7g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8A">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8Q">]</span></span></a></sup> A surviving letter from 20 May 1943, when Arkady was studying at an artillery school, mentions their joint novella about the adventures of their mother and younger brother in blockaded Leningrad ("Write to me in more detail about how your work on the novella is going and what plan you're following"). At that time, Arkady was seventeen, and Boris was ten.<sup about="#mwt96" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis200848_28-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis200848&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200848-28&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;48&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis200848-28" id="mw8g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9A">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw9Q">]</span></span></a></sup> Surviving texts in the archive date to a later period, particularly Arkady Strugatsky's service in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamchatka_Peninsula" title="Kamchatka Peninsula" id="mw9g">Kamchatka</a>, which left him with free time.<sup about="#mwt99" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016146_29-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016146&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016146-29&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;146&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016146-29" id="mw9w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-Q">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-g">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw-w">The Strugatsky Brothers' literary "godfather" was Roman Kim, a counterintelligence officer specializing in Japan, who was a well-known Soviet adventure genre writer. Biographers differ on when their acquaintance occurred. In the brothers' 1952 correspondence, Kim's novella "The Notebook Found in Sunchon" is mentioned, which Arkady strongly recommended to Boris. Their direct acquaintance may have taken place in 1954 or 1958 (the latter date has documentary evidence).<sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008141–143_30-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis2008141–143&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008141–143-30&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;141–143&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008141–143-30" id="mw_A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_g">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_w">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt105" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEProsvetov20153,_211_31-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt108" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331_32-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331-32&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Kulanov&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;331&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331-32" id="mwAQQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQY">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt111" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBurya2018_33-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEBurya2018&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurya2018-33&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Burya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2018&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurya2018-33" id="mwAQg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQo">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1954, Arkady Strugatsky wrote the documentary novella <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashes_of_Bikini_Atoll?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Ashes of Bikini Atoll (page does not exist)" about="#mwt114" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwAQw" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Interlanguage link&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Interlanguage_link&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ashes of Bikini Atoll&quot;},&quot;lt&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Ashes of Bikini Atoll&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Пепел Бикини&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Ashes of Bikini Atoll&quot;]}}">Ashes of Bikini Atoll</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt114" id="mwAQ0"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Пепел%20Бикини" title="ru:Пепел Бикини" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span> (about the fate of the fishing schooner <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daigo_Fukuryū_Maru" title="Daigo Fukuryū Maru" id="mwAQ4">Fukuryu Maru</a>), which his colleague, Sovinformburo employee Lev Petrov, took on to promote. Petrov managed to arrange its publication in the journal "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_East_(journal)?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Far East (journal) (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" id="mwAQ8" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;Far East (journal)&quot;]}}">Far East</a>," and in April 1956, Roman Kim became the editor of the novella. According to researcher Viktor Petrovich Burya, Arkady Strugatsky likely participated in editing the novella, suggesting their acquaintance with Kim dates to April 1956. The decision to publish was made in August 1956, with the author's fee split equally; by then, both Strugatsky and Petrov had settled in Moscow.<sup about="#mwt115" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBurya2018_33-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEBurya2018&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurya2018-33&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Burya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2018&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurya2018-33" id="mwARA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARI">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARM">]</span></span></a></sup> Roman Kim oversaw the science fiction direction in the Writers' Union and, in 1963, actively supported the Strugatskys, emphasizing that "the West is waging a broad <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Sovietism" title="Anti-Sovietism" id="mwARQ">anti-Soviet</a> and anti-communist offensive in science fiction." Accordingly, the Strugatskys considered him one of the key figures in the rise of Soviet science fiction.<sup about="#mwt118" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331_32-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331-32&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Kulanov&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;331&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulanov2016331-32" id="mwARU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARc">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwARk">In 1958, while the Strugatskys awaited the approval of their debut novella <i id="mwARo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Land_of_Crimson_Clouds" title="The Land of Crimson Clouds" id="mwARs">The Land of Crimson Clouds</a></i> at the "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detskaya_Literatura" title="Detskaya Literatura" id="mwARw">Detgiz</a>" publishing house, Roman Kim was organizing a specialized almanac of science fiction and adventure and commissioned Arkady Strugatsky to write the novella <i id="mwAR0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_Inside_(Strugatsky's_novella)?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="From the Inside (Strugatsky's novella) (page does not exist)" about="#mwt121" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwAR4" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Interlanguage link&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Interlanguage_link&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;From the Inside (Strugatsky's novella)&quot;},&quot;lt&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;From the Inside&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;ru&quot;},&quot;3&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Извне (повесть)&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}" data-mw-i18n="{&quot;title&quot;:{&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;x-page&quot;,&quot;key&quot;:&quot;red-link-title&quot;,&quot;params&quot;:[&quot;From the Inside (Strugatsky's novella)&quot;]}}">From the Inside</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt121" id="mwAR8"><span typeof="mw:Entity">&nbsp;</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/Извне%20(повесть)" title="ru:Извне (повесть)" class="extiw">ru</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span></i>. At that time, they also met Ivan Yefremov. In December 1960, Roman Kim, Ivan Yefremov, and critic K. Andreev provided recommendations for the Strugatskys' admission to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Writers" title="Union of Soviet Writers" id="mwASA">Union of Soviet Writers</a>.<sup about="#mwt122" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-36" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-36&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;refn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Refn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;In Moscow's literary life in the 1950s, informal associations like salons played a significant role; A. N. Strugatsky was initially a member of translator [[Vera Nikolaevna Markova|Vera Markova]]’s salon, where Roman Kim was a regular and where the acquaintance with Yefremov occurred.{{sfn|Skalandis|2008|p=118}}{{sfn|Chernyakhovskaya|2016|p=131}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-36" data-mw-group="Notes" id="mwASE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASM">[</span>Notes 3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The admission process was delayed for several reasons (despite the adventure and science fiction subsection recommending acceleration twice), and only on 25 February 1964, were Arkady and Boris Strugatsky elected to the Writers' Union with nine votes in favor and five abstentions. Secondary recommendations were provided by Ivan Yefremov, K. Andreev, and A. Gromova.<sup about="#mwt131" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEProsvetov2015212_37-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEProsvetov2015212&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEProsvetov2015212-37&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Prosvetov&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2015&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;212&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEProsvetov2015212-37" id="mwASU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASc">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt134" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016131_35-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016131&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016131-35&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;p&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;131&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016131-35" id="mwASk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASs">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAS0">After 1966–1968, the attitude toward science fiction (especially the philosophical science fiction developed by the Strugatskys) changed at the level of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agitprop" title="Agitprop" id="mwAS4">Department of Agitation and Propaganda</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Committee_of_the_CPSU" title="Central Committee of the CPSU" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAS8">Central Committee of the CPSU</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Committee_of_the_Komsomol" title="Central Committee of the Komsomol" id="mwATA">Central Committee of the Komsomol</a>. This was expressed through closed resolutions regulating publishing policies in science fiction and critical campaigns in the press against specific writers and works. Serious conflicts between the co-authors and party authorities occurred in 1968 and 1972 regarding the publication of the novella <i id="mwATE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tale_of_the_Troika" title="Tale of the Troika" id="mwATI">Tale of the Troika</a></i> in the almanac <i id="mwATM">Angara</i> and the printing of the novella <i id="mwATQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ugly_Swans" title="The Ugly Swans" id="mwATU">The Ugly Swans</a></i> in the émigré publication <i id="mwATY">Grani</i>.<sup about="#mwt137" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016264–265_38-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016264–265&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016264–265-38&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;264–265&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016264–265-38" id="mwATc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATk">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATo">]</span></span></a></sup> After 1968, three of the Strugatskys' works (<i id="mwATs">Tale of the Troika</i>, <i id="mwATw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snail_on_the_Slope" title="Snail on the Slope" id="mwAT0">Snail on the Slope</a></i>, and <i id="mwAT4">The Ugly Swans</i>) gradually became uncensored literature, circulated in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samizdat" title="Samizdat" id="mwAT8">samizdat</a>. The Strugatskys' attempts to distance themselves from publications in European émigré outlets were largely unsuccessful, even despite a rebuttal in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literaturnaya_Gazeta" title="Literaturnaya Gazeta" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAUA">Literaturnaya Gazeta</a> in 1972. Each such publication further distanced the authors from official print media.<sup about="#mwt140" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016269–272_39-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016269–272&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016269–272-39&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Chernyakhovskaya&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2016&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;269–272&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChernyakhovskaya2016269–272-39" id="mwAUE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt143" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-40&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;refn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Refn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Notes&quot;},&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;In the 1970s, science fiction split into \&quot;official\&quot; and \&quot;samizdat\&quot; categories. The former existed under the control of the Central Committee of the CPSU, with official publication and distribution channels being the journal ''Iskatel'' and the new editorial board of [[Molodaya Gvardiya (publisher)|Molodaya Gvardiya]] led by Yuri Medvedev.{{sfn|Chernyakhovskaya|2016|pp=269–272}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-40" data-mw-group="Notes" id="mwAUU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUc">[</span>Notes 4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUg">]</span></span></a></sup> A landmark event for the Strugatskys in their relations with the authorities and Soviet publishing policy in science fiction was the publication history of the novella <i id="mwAUk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roadside_Picnic" title="Roadside Picnic" id="mwAUo">Roadside Picnic</a>,</i> which dragged on for ten years. In 1970, the authors submitted an application to <i id="mwAUs">Molodaya Gvardiya</i> for the collection <i id="mwAUw">Unappointed Encounters</i> (<i id="mwAU0">From the Inside, The Kid, Roadside Picnic</i>), which was published in 1980 after repeated reminders and even lawsuits.<sup about="#mwt149" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008373–374_41-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis2008373–374&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008373–374-41&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;373–374&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008373–374-41" id="mwAU4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVA">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVE">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1974, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGB" title="KGB" id="mwAVI">Committee for State Security</a> showed interest in Boris Strugatsky in connection with the case of his friend Mikhail Kheifets, arrested for distributing his article <i id="mwAVM">Joseph Brodsky and Our Generatio</i>n (a preface to the so-called <i id="mwAVQ">Maramzin</i> collection of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Alexandrovich_Brodsky" title="Joseph Alexandrovich Brodsky" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAVU">Brodsky</a>'s works). Boris Strugatsky and his wife Adelaide (née Karpelyuk) were witnesses. The emotional experiences were reflected in the tone and plot idea of the novella <i id="mwAVY">A Billion Years Before the End of the World</i>.<sup about="#mwt152" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008392–394_42-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw="{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;group&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FOOTNOTESkalandis2008392–394&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008392–394-42&quot;},&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;sfn&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Sfn&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;Skalandis&quot;},&quot;2&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;2008&quot;},&quot;pp&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;392–394&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESkalandis2008392–394-42" id="mwAVc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVk">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVo">]</span></span></a></sup> Although in the 1960s–1970s, the Strugatskys (like I. A.
